### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:7:1)


**וְלוּ הוֹאַלְנוּ** הַלְוַאי נִמְלַכְנוּ לָשֶׁבֶת בְּעֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן מִזְרָחָה, בְּאֶרֶץ סִיחוֹן וְעוֹג שֶׁנִכְבְּשָׁה כְּבָר.


**We should have been content.** We should have decided to dwell on the eastern side of the Jordan, in the land of Sichon and Og which has already been captured.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:9:1)


**וּמַה תַּעֲשֶׂה לְשִׁמְךָ** הַמְשׁוּתָּף בִּשְׁמֵנוּ, זֶהוּ מִדְרָשׁוֹ. וּפְשׁוּטוֹ וּמַה תַּעֲשֶׂה לְשֵׁם גְּבוּרָתְךָ שֶׁיָּצָא כְּבָר, וְעַתָּה יֹאמְרוּ תָּשַׁשׁ כֹּחוֹ.


**And what will you do for Your Name, which is associated with our name?²** This is the Aggadic interpretation. The simple meaning is, What will you do for your mighty Name, that has already achieved such fame; and now they will be able to say, His strength has become lessened.

footnotes:
² God’s Name, אֵל is part of our name יִשְׂרָאֵל.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:10:1)


**קֻם לָךְ** קָם לָךְ כְּתִיב, עָמַד לְךָ מַה שֶּׁהִתְפַּלַּלְתָּ לְפָנַי וְהִזְכַּרְתָּ. דָּבָר אַחֵר: עָמַדְתָּ לְךָ בַּמַּחֲנֶה וְלֹא יָצָאתָ עִמָּהֶם, וַאֲנִי אָמַרְתִּי: (במדבר כז יז), אֲשֶׁר יוֹצִיאֵם וַאֲשֶׁר יְבִיאֵם, (דברים לא כג): כִּי אַתָּה תָּבִיא אֶת בְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל... וְאָנֹכִי אֶהְיֶה עִמְּךָ: (שם ג כח), כִּי הוּא יַעֲבֹר לִפְנֵי הָעָם הַזֶּה וְהוּא יַנְחִיל אוֹתָם אִם תֵּלֵךְ אַתָּה לִפְנֵיהֶם יַצְלִיחוּ, וְאִם לָאו לֹא יַצְלִיחוּ. דָּבָר אַחֵר: **קֻם לָךְ** בִּשְׁבִילְךָ זֹאת לָהֶם, לֹא אָמַרְתִּי לְךָ לְהַקְדִּישׁ שְׁלַל הָעִיר.


**Get yourself up.** [Even though we read the word as קֻם], it is written קם [which could be read קָם]. [The explanation therefore would be as follows]: Your prayers to Me have stood up for you, and [everything] you mentioned.³ Another explanation: you stood yourself in the camp and did not go out with them,⁴ and I had said, ‘Who will take them out [to war] and who will bring them [back].’⁵ ‘For you will bring the Bnei Yisroel— and I will be with you.’⁶ ‘For he will cross over before this people, and he will spur them on to inherit.’⁷ If you go before them, they will succeed, and if not, they will not succeed. Another explanation: Get *yourself* up, it is your fault [because of you] that this has happened to them. I did not tell you to sanctify the booty of the city.

footnotes:
³ Your prayers and everything you said have accomplished their purpose. I will not permit the people to be defeated.
⁴ When you sent the men to capture the city of Ai, you remained at the camp and did not lead them.
⁵ Bamidbar 27:17.
⁶ Devorim 31:23.
⁷ Devorim 3:28.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:14:1)


**לַבָּתִּים** יֵשׁ בְּמִשְׁפָּחָה אַחַת הַרְבֵּה בָּתֵּי אָבוֹת.


**Households.** In one family grouping there can be many individual households.


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:14:2)


**לַגְּבָרִים** לְרָאשֵׁי גּוּלְגּוֹלֶת, כָּל אַנְשֵׁי הַבַּיִת יָבֹאוּ לַגּוֹרָל.


**Men** Individualy, man by man. Each man of the household was to come to the choosing of lots.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:15:1)


**(תוספת) יִשָּׂרֵף בָּאֵשׁ** הָאֹהֶל וְהַמִּטַּלְטְלִין.


**Burned with fire.** His tent and movable property [shall be burned].


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:15:2)


**אֹתוֹ וְאֶת כָּל אֲשֶׁר לוֹ** כַּדִּין הַמְפוֹרָשׁ לְּמַטָּה (להלן פסוק כה): אוֹתוֹ וְהַבְּהֵמָה בִּסְקִילָה, וְהַזָּקֵף שֶׁהוּא עַל 'בָּאֵשׁ' מוֹכִיחַ שֶׁהוּא נִפְרָד 'מֵאוֹתוֹ', שֶׁכֵּן מָצִינוּ מִקְרָאוֹת שֶׁהַטַּעַם מְחַלְּקָן, כְּמוֹ (דברים יא ל): אַחֲרֵי דֶּרֶךְ מְבוֹא הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ, שֶׁ'אַחֲרֵי' מוּפְלָג 'מִדֶּרֶךְ' עַל יְדֵי הַטַּעַם, וְכֵן פִּתְרוֹנוֹ: הַנִּלְכָּד בַּחֵרֶם יִשָּׂרֵף בָּאֵשׁ, הָרָאוּי לִישָּׂרֵף כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּפֹרָשׁ לְּמַטָּה.


**He and all that he possesses.** [This should be understood] according to the law explained below— he and his livestock [are put to death] by stoning.⁸ The *zakeif* accent, appearing over the word, בָּאֵשׁ indicates that the word בָּאֵשׁ (with fire) is separated from the word אוֹתוֹ, (he). We find other verses in which the accent divides the verse, such as, ‘אַחַרֵי דֶרֶך מְבוֹא הַשֶׁמֶשׁ’⁹ (Far past the Jordan, the direction in which the sun sets). The word אַחַרֵי is separated from the word דֶרֶך by virtue of the [*pashta*] accent. Thus the interpretation of our verse is: He who is convicted [by the Ark] because of his taking of the condemned, shall be burned with fire, [which means] that which is fit to be burned [shall be burned], as is explained below.¹⁰

footnotes:
⁸ See below v. 25.
⁹ Devorim 11:30. See Rashi and Sifsei Chachomim there.
¹⁰ V. 25.


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:15:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:15:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:15:3)


**אֹתוֹ וְאֶת כָּל אֲשֶׁר לוֹ** כַּדִּין הָאָמוּר לְּמַטָּה, וְזֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר, וְדוֹמֶה לוֹ (שמואל ב ה ח): כָּל מַכֵּה יְבֻסִי וְיִגַּע בַּצִּנּוֹר, לֹא פֵּירַשׁ מַה יִּהְיֶה לוֹ. וּבְדִבְרֵי הַיָּמִים (א יא ו) פֵּירַשׁ: יִהְיֶה לְרֹאשׁ, אַף כָּאן לֹא פֵּירַשׁ מַה יִּהְיֶה בְּ'אוֹתוֹ וְכָל אֲשֶׁר לוֹ' וּלְמַטָּה פֵּירַשׁ.


**He and all that he possesses.** [This should be understood.] According to the law explained below.¹¹ This is an abbreviated verse, and there is a similar verse to it: ‘Whoever strikes the Jebusites and reaches the tower.’¹² The verse does not explain what will be done to him,¹³ but in Sefer Divrei Ha Yomim,¹⁴ it is explained ‘that he will become a leader.’ Here too, it is not explained what will be done to him ‘and to all that he possesses,’ but everything is explained below.¹⁵

footnotes:
¹¹ Ibid.
¹² II Shmuel 5:8.
¹³ We are not told what reward will be given to the one who reaches the tower.
¹⁴ I Divrei Hayumim 11:5.
¹⁵ V. 25.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:16:1)


**וַיַּקְרֵב אֶת יִשְׂרָאֵל** לִפְנֵי הַחֹשֶׁן, מָקוֹם שֶׁהַשְּׁבָטִים כְּתוּבִים, וּמָסַר לוֹ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא סִימָן, שֶׁהַשֵּׁבֶט שֶׁחָטָא אַבְנוֹ כָּהָה, וְכָהֲתָה אַבְנוֹ שֶׁל יְהוּדָה.


**And brought the Bnei Yisroel near.** He brought them in front of the *Choshen*¹⁶ was one of the sacred garments worn by the *Kohein Gadol.* The Divine decisions were given through the letters inscribed on the *Choshen.* on which the names of the tribes were inscribed. The Holy One, blessed is He, gave him a sign— the stone of the sinning tribe will fade, and the stone inscribed with Yehudah’s name, faded.

footnotes:
¹⁶ The Choshen



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:17:1)


**וַיַּקְרֵב אֶת מִשְׁפַּחַת יְהוּדָה** לְגוֹרָל אֶחָד קֵרַב רָאשֵׁי הַמִּשְׁפָּחוֹת, אָדָם אֶחָד מִכָּל הַמִּשְׁפָּחָה וְהִטִּילוּ גּוֹרָל עַל מִי יִפּוֹל, וְאַחַר כָּךְ עַל כָּל בָּתֵּי אָבוֹת שֶׁל אוֹתָהּ מִשְׁפָּחָה, וּבָא אָדָם אֶחָד מִכָּל בֵּית אָב לְגוֹרָל, וְאַחַר כֵּן בָּאוּ כָּל אַנְשֵׁי אוֹתוֹ הַבַּיִת.


**And when he brought near the family of Yehudah.** To the initial casting of lots he brought the heads of families near, one man from each family, and they cast lots [to see] on whom it would fall. Afterwards lots were cast on each household— one man from each household came to the casting of lots, and then came all the men of that particular household.



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:19:1)


**שִׂים־נָא כָבוֹד** הִתְחִיל לְהוֹצִיא לַעַז עַל הַגּוֹרָל, אָמַר לוֹ: בְּגוֹרָל אַתָּה בָּא עָלַי, אַתָּה וְאֶלְעָזָר הַכֹּהֵן גְּדוֹלֵי הַדּוֹר אַתֶּם; הַטֵּל הַגּוֹרָל עַל שְׁנֵיכֶם, וְיִפּוֹל עַל הָאֶחָד. אָמַר לוֹ: שִׂים־נָא כָבוֹד, בְּבַקָּשָׁה מִמְּךָ, אַל תּוֹצִיא לַעַז עַל הַגּוֹרָלוֹת שֶׁבּוֹ עֲתִידָה הָאָרֶץ לֵיחָלֵק.


**I beseech you, give honor.** Ochan began to defame the casting of lots.¹⁷ He said to Yehoshua, “On the basis of casting lots, Are you accusing me? You and Elozer, the *Kohein,* are the greatest of the generation. If a lot was cast between you, it would certainly fall on one of you.”¹⁸ Yehoshua then said to him, ‘I beseech you, give honor, (etc).’ “I ask you, please do not defame the method of casting lots, for this is the way the land will ultimately be divided.”¹⁹

footnotes:
¹⁷ As a means of establishing guilt.
¹⁸ Therefore lots are not a definitive method of reaching a decision.
¹⁹ You are therefore casting doubt on the division of the land.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:20:1)


**וַיַּעַן עָכָן** רָאָה בְּנֵי יְהוּדָה נֶאֱסָפִים לְמִלְחָמָה, אָמַר: מוּטָב שֶׁאָמוּת אֲנִי לְבַדִּי וְאַל יֵהָרְגוּ כַּמָּה אֲלָפִים מִיִּשְׂרָאֵל.


**And Ochan answered.** He saw the men of [the tribe of] Yehudah assembling for war,²⁰ and he said, “It is better that I alone should die rather than have many thousands of Bnei Yisroel killed.”²¹

footnotes:
²⁰ They were prepared to go to war to protect Ochan, who was one of their tribe.
²¹ There would have been a civil war between the tribe of Yehudah and the other tribes. This would have caused thousands of casualties.


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:20:2)


**וְכָזֹאת וְכָזֹאת עָשִׂיתִי** גַּם בַּחֲרָמִים אֲחֵרִים בִּימֵי משֶׁה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (במדבר כא ב): וְהַחֲרַמְתִּי אֶת עָרֵיהֶם.


**And I have done the following.** (Lit. and this and this have I done)²² I also sinned regarding other sacred things, during the days of Moshe, as it is said, ‘I will sanctify their cities.’²³

footnotes:
²² The implication was that this was not the only time he had sinned regarding sacred things.
²³ Bamidbar 21:2. This occurred when the Bnei Yisroel took revenge against Midian and declared that some of the booty be considered sacred.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:21:1)


**וָאֵרֶא בַשָּׁלָל** הִתְבּוֹנַנְתִּי בְּמַה שֶּׁכָּתוּב בַּתּוֹרָה (דברים כ יד): וְאָכַלְתָּ [אֶת־שְׁלַל] וְגוֹ'.


**I saw amidst the spoils**—I gave thought to what is written in the Torah,²⁴ understands וָאֵרֶה as referring to intellectual comprehension. ‘and you shall consume, [the booty] etc.’²⁵

footnotes:
²⁴ Rashi
²⁵ Devorim 20:14. Ochan took this verse as a command to consume the booty captured from the enemy.


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:21:2)


**אַדֶּרֶת שִׁנְעָר** תַּרְגּוּמוֹ: אִיצְטְלָא (דְּמֵילָתָא) בַּבְלִי, שֶׁכָּל מֶלֶךְ שֶׁלֹּא קָנָה לוֹ פַּלְטֵרִין בְּאֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל, לֹא נִתְקָרְרָה דַּעְתּוֹ בְּמַלְכוּתוֹ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (ירמיהו ג יט): וְאֶתֵּן־לָךְ אֶרֶץ חֶמְדָּה נַחֲלַת צְבִי צִבְאוֹת גּוֹיִם, וְהָיָה לְמֶלֶךְ בָּבֶל פַּלְטֵרִין בִּירִיחוֹ, וּכְשֶׁהָיָה בָּא לְכָאן לוֹבֵשׁ אוֹתָם.


**A Babylonian garment.** This is translated according to the Targum. Any king who did not possess a palace in Eretz Yisroel, was not content with his kingship, as it is said, ‘And I will give you a desired land, and inheritance desired by many nations.’²⁶ The king of Babylonia had a palace in Yericho, and when he visited here he would wear them.²⁷

footnotes:
²⁶ Yirmiahu 3:19. The verse obviously refers to Eretz Yisroel, which is the land desired many kings.
²⁷ This explains how a Babylonian garment was found in Yericho.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:22:1)


**וַיָּרֻצוּ הָאֹהֱלָה** שֶׁלֹּא יַקְדִּימוּ שֵׁבֶט יְהוּדָה וְיִטְּלוּהָ מִשָּׁם לְהַכְחִישׁ אֶת הַגּוֹרָל.


**And they ran to [his] tent.** They wanted to get there before the tribe of Yehudah had a chance to remove the articles and deny the accuracy of the lots.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:23:1)


**וַיַּצִּקֻם** (תַּרְגּוּם:) וְאַתִּיכוּנוּן, וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (סנהדרין מד א): בָּא וַחֲבָטָן לִפְנֵי הַמָּקוֹם, אָמַר לְפָנָיו: רִבּוֹנוֹ שֶׁל עוֹלָם, עַל אֵלּוּ יִפְּלוּ רוּבָּן שֶׁל סַנְהֶדְרִין.


**And they spread them out.** The Targum renders וַיַצִקָם as וְאַתִּיכוּנוּן [which implies, and they melted them] Our Sages said, ‘He [Yehoshua] came and cast them before the Omnipresent. He said before him, “Master of the universe, for these, shall the bulk of Sanhedrin fall?’”²⁸

footnotes:
²⁸ Because of these insignificant items, will You punish them.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:24:1)


**וְאֶת בָּנָיו וְגוֹ' וְכָל יִשְׂרָאֵל** לִרְאוֹת בְּרִדּוּיוֹ וְיִוַּסְּרוּ מִלַּעֲשׂוֹת כָּמוֹהוּ.


**His sons etc., and all of Yisroel.**²⁹ To witness his downfall, and to be forewarned against doing as he did.

footnotes:
²⁹ Why was it necessary to bring all of Yisroel? Rashi therefore explains—to witness his downfall.


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:24:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:24:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:24:2)


**וְאֶת שׁוֹרוֹ וְאֶת חֲמֹרוֹ** לְאַבְּדָם, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (לעיל פסוק טו): וְהָיָה הַנִּלְכָּד בַּחֵרֶם יִשָּׂרֵף בָּאֵשׁ אֹתוֹ וְכָל אֲשֶׁר לוֹ.


**His ox(en) and his mule(s)** to destroy them, as it was said, ‘And he who is convicted by [his guilt in taking of] the sacred things, shall be burned with fire, he and all that he possesses.’³⁰

footnotes:
³⁰ See Verse 15 above, and Rashi’s commentary there.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on Joshua 7:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:25:1)


**וַיִּרְגְּמוּ אֹתוֹ... אֶבֶן** שֶׁחִלֵּל אֶת הַשַּׁבָּת.


**And [they] stoned him.**³¹ He had desecrated the Shabbos.

footnotes:
³¹ Why this particular death? Rashi answers. he desecrated the Shabbos.


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:25:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:25:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:25:2)


**וַיִּשְׂרְפוּ אֹתָם** הָאֹהֶל וְהַמִּטַּלְטְלִין.


**And they burned them.** This refers to the tent, and all other movable objects.


###### Rashi on Joshua 7:25:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:25:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/7:25:3)


**וַיִּסְקְלוּ אֹתָם** הַשּׁוֹר וְהַבְּהֵמָה.


**And they stoned them.** This refers to the ox(en) and other animals.



## Chapter 8


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:2:1)


**תָּבֹזּוּ לָכֶם** וְאַל תַּחֲרִימוּ הַשָּׁלָל עוֹד.


**Take for yourselves as booty.** You shall no longer consecrate the spoils.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:6:1)


**הַתִּיקֵנוּ אוֹתָם** לְשׁוֹן תִּיק, שֶׁנּוֹצִיאֵם מִתִּיק הָעִיר, דישפורי"ר בְּלַעַז (לְהוֹצִיא, לִשְׁלוֹף). וְיֵשׁ לְפוֹתְרוֹ לְשׁוֹן נְתִיקָה, כְּמוֹ (ירמיהו יב ג): הַתִּיקֵם כְּצֹאן לְטִבְחָה.


**Until we have drawn them.** הַתִּיקֵנוּ is an expression of תִּיק, a pocket, i.e., until we have drawn them from the pocket of the city.¹ Defaurer in old french. We may also explain it as an expression of נְתִּיקָה, drawing out similar to ‘Draw them out like sheep to the slaughter.’²

footnotes:
¹ The city provided them with protection, as a pocket protects its contents.
² Yirmiahu 12:3.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:7:1)


**וְהוֹרַשְׁתֶּם** (תַּרְגּוּם:) וּתְתָרְכוּן.


**And drive out.**³

footnotes:
³ This is translated according to the Targum who renders the word הוֹרַשְׁתֶם as תְתַרְכוּן which means to drive out.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:9:1)


**מִיָּם לָעַי** שֶׁהָעַי מִקֶּדֶם לְבֵית אֵל, וּבֵית אֵל מִיָּם לָעַי.


**To the west of Ai.** Ai was east of Beis-eil and Beis-eil was west of Ai.


###### Rashi on Joshua 8:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:9:2)


**וַיָּלֶן יְהוֹשֻׁעַ בְּתוֹךְ הָעָם** לִהְיוֹת נָכוֹן בְּהַשְׁכָּמַת הַבֹּקֶר.


**And Yehoshua rested—in the midst of the people.** To be ready early in the morning.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:10:1)


**וַיַּעַל הוּא** כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַמָּקוֹם, אִם הוּא עוֹבֵר לִפְנֵיהֶם עוֹבְרִין, וְאִם לָאו אֵין עוֹבְרִין.


**And he went up.** He fulfilled that which God had said to him, “If he goes at their head, they will cross, and if not, they will not cross.”



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:12:1)


**וַיִּקַּח כַּחֲמֵשֶׁת אֲלָפִים אִישׁ** אוֹרֵב אַחַר אוֹרֵב, אֶחָד קָרוֹב לָעִיר מֵחֲבֵירוֹ.


**He took about five thousand men.** One ambush contingent following the other each approaching nearer to the city than its fellow.⁴

footnotes:
⁴ The question is, what was the purpose of this second ambush party? Some suggest that these five thousand men were not to enter the city. Their task was to attack the army of Ai from behind, as they were pursuing the main army of Bnei Yisroel. When Yehoshua and his army would turn and attack, the army of Ai would be caught between them.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:13:1)


**וַיָּשִׂימוּ הָעָם** שִׂימָה זוֹ לְשׁוֹן הַזְמָנָה, סָמוּךְ לַחוֹמָה לְהִלָּחֵם, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּבֶן הֲדַד (מלכים א כ ל): שִׂימוּ עַל הָעִיר, וַיָּשִׂימוּ.


**The people positioned.** This positioning expresses readiness to wage war near the [city] wall. This is similar to what is said regarding Ben-Hadad, in I Melachim 20:12, (who said to his servants:) “Position yourselves against the city and they positioned themselves.”


###### Rashi on Joshua 8:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:13:2)


**וְאֶת עֲקֵבוֹ** וְאֶת אָרְבּוֹ, כְּמוֹ (בראשית כז לו): וַיַּעְקְבֵנִי.


**And their ambush party** their ambushers this word עַקֵבוֹ is similar to וַיעֲקְבֵנִי.⁵

footnotes:
⁵ Bereishis 27:36. Eisov said regarding Yaakov, וַיַעְקְבֵנִי זֶה פַּעֲמַיִם “He has deceived me twice.” An ambush implies cunning and trickery.


###### Rashi on Joshua 8:13:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:13:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:13:3)


**וַיֵּלֶךְ יְהוֹשֻׁעַ בַּלַּיְלָה הַהוּא בְּתוֹךְ הָעֵמֶק** אָמְרוּ רַבּוֹתֵינוּ (סנהדרין מד ב) שֶׁלָּן בְּעוֹמְקָהּ שֶׁל הֲלָכָה.


**That night, Yehoshua walked through the valley** Our Sages have said that he lodged in the depth of the law.⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Our Sages interpreted the word עֵמֶק as an expression of depth, and they say he spent the night studying Torah. After all the preparations were made and orders given, Yehoshua had time to return to the study of Torah, which is the real protection in time of crisis. He remembered the rebuke of the angel on the night before the fall of Yericho.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:14:1)


**לַמּוֹעֵד** לִזְמַן הַיּוֹם, שֶׁנּוֹעֲצוּ יַחַד מֵאֶתְמוֹל בְּאוֹתוֹ שָׁעָה נֵצֵא, שֶׁהָיוּ מְנַחֲשִׁים וּמְעוֹנְנִים.


**At the appointed time.** At the time of day they had astrologically predicted previously, saying “at that time we will go out,” for they were sorcerers and diviners.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:15:1)


**וַיִּנָּגְעוּ** לְשׁוֹן נֶגַע (כְּתַרְגּוּמוֹ:) וְאִתְּבַרוּ, הֶרְאוּ עַצְמָן כְּאִלּוּ הֵם נִגָּפִים לִפְנֵיהֶם.


**Pretended to be stricken.** This is an expression of נֶגַע, plague, or beating. They gave the appearance of being beaten before them.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:18:1)


**נְטֵה בַּכִּידוֹן** הוּא הָיָה סִימָן לָאוֹרֵב לָצֵאת מִן הַמַּאֲרָב, בִּרְאוֹתוֹ הַכִּידוֹן נָטוּי עַל הָעִיר. **כִּידוֹן** שפיד"ו בְּלַעַז (רוֹמַח, חַנִית).


**Stretch out the spear.** This was the signal to the ambush party.⁷ They were to leave the ambush when they saw the spear stretched out toward the city. כִּידוֹן is espida, a sword.

footnotes:
⁷ This signal was for the second, smaller ambush party of 5000 men. The 30,000 men were too far away to see Yehoshua give the signal to attack. The second troop was in a closer position and would be able to see his signal.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:20:1)


**יָדַיִם** כֹּחַ.


**יָדַיִם** hands, here refers to strength.


###### Rashi on Joshua 8:20:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:20:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:20:2)


**וְהָעָם הַנָּס הַמִּדְבָּר** יִשְׂרָאֵל שֶׁנָּסוּ אֶל הַמִּדְבָּר כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמַר לְמַעְלָה, נֶהְפַּךְ לְהִלָּחֵם אֶל הָרוֹדֵף.


**The people who fled to the desert.** The Bnei Yisroel who had fled to the desert, as stated above (v.15) turned back to battle the pursuers.



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:22:1)


**וְאֵלֶּה יָצְאוּ מִן הָעִיר** הָאוֹרֵב שֶׁהִצִּית אֶת הָעִיר.


**And the others came out of the city.** The “others” refers to the ambush party that had set fire to the city.



### Verse 30

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:30:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:30:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:30:1)


**אָז יִבְנֶה וְגוֹ'** פָּרָשָׁה זוֹ כְּתוּבָה מוּקְדָּם וּמְאוּחָר, שֶׁמִּיּוֹם שֶׁעָבְרוּ אֶת הַיַּרְדֵּן עָשָׂה כֵּן.


**Then [Yehoshua] built.** This *parasha* is not in chronoligical order, [for Yehoshua built the altar] on the day they crossed the Jordan river.⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Since the following verse states that he built the altar as Moshe had commanded them, we must conclude that this verse describes events that occured before the conquest of Yericho.



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:32:1)


**וַיִּכְתָּב־שָׁם עַל הָאֲבָנִים** הֵן הֵנָּה הָאֲבָנִים הָאֲמוּרִים לְמַעְלָה, לְאַחַר מַעֲשֶׂה זֶה קִפְּלוּ (קלפו) הַסִּיד מֵעֲלֵיהֶם וֶהֱבִיאוּם הַגִּלְגָּל.


**And there he wrote on the stones.**⁹ There are the same stones mentioned above.(4:20) After the altar was built, they scraped off the whitewash¹⁰it was written. According to Rabbi Yehudah it written directly on the stones, and later whitewashed as the verse states, “you are to write on them—and coat them with whitewash.” (Devorim 27:3,4.) Rabbi Shimon said that if they were whitewashed on top of the writing no one would be able to read it, which would defeat the purpose of the commandment. According to him the whitewash was put on first, to make the stones easier to write on and easier to read. (See Maseches Sotah 35). and brought them to Gilgal.¹¹

footnotes:
⁹ There are different opinions as to what Yehoshua wrote on the stones. Some say that he wrote only Seifer Devorim which is itself the repetition of the Torah, as Moshe taught the Bnei Yisroel in the last year of the forty years in the wilderness—Seifer Devorim is referred to as מִשְׁנֵה תּוֹרָה, which means the repetition or copy of the Torah, which are the exact words used in this verse. Others say that he only wrote the Ten Commandments as they are recorded in Seifer Devorim (5:6-18). Most commentators maintain that Yehoshua wrote the entire Torah from beginning to end, and that it was a literal copy of the Torah.
¹⁰ There are different opinions concerning how
¹¹ See Devorim 27:2 and Rashi’s comments there.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on Joshua 8:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/8:33:1)


**לְבָרֵךְ אֶת הָעָם יִשְׂרָאֵל בָּרִאשׁוֹנָה** לְהַקְדִּים בְּרָכוֹת לִקְלָלוֹת: בָּרוּךְ הָאִישׁ אֲשֶׁר לֹא יַעֲשֶׂה פֶסֶל וּמַסֵּכָה.


**To bless the people of Yisroel first.** To pronounce the blessings before the curses,¹² began to recite the blessings and the curses. Although the Torah mentions only the curses, each curse was actually preceded by a corresponding blessing. (See Rashi to Devorim 27:12). “Blessed is the man who will not make a statue or a molten image.”

footnotes:
¹² When all the tribes were assembled (as described in Devorim 27:12,13,) the Levi’im



## Chapter 9


### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Joshua 9:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:1)


**וַיַּעֲשׂוּ גַּם הֵמָּה בְּעָרְמָה** כְּמוֹ שֶׁעָשׂוּ בְּנֵי יַעֲקֹב בְּעָרְמָה (בראשית לד) וּבַחֲמוֹר אֲבִי שְׁכֶם שֶׁהָיָה חִוִּי, וְיוֹשְׁבֵי גִּבְעוֹן מִן הַחִוִּי הָיוּ, כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמוּר בָּעִנְיָן.


**And they too¹ proceded with deception** just as the sons of Yaakov dealt with trickery with Chamor, the father of Shechem, who was a Chivite.² The people of Givon were descendents of the Chivites, as it is said in this context.

footnotes:
¹ Our verse indicates that others had also practiced deception but does not tell us who it was. Rashi proceeds to tell us who else had deceived them.
² The Givonim were a branch of Chivites, who were related Shechem who had raped Dinah the daughter of Yaakov. They remembered how their relatives were tricked by the sons of Yaakov and were subsequently killed by the two son of Yaakov. Thus the Gevonim felt justified in perpetrating their deception. (See Bereishis 34:1-26).


###### Rashi on Joshua 9:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:2)


**וַיִּצְטַיָּרוּ** עָשׂוּ עַצְמָם כְּהוֹלְכִים בִּשְׁלִיחוּת, לְשׁוֹן (ירמיהו מט יד): וְצִיר בַּגּוֹיִם שֻׁלַּח, וְכָל תֵּיבָה שֶׁתְּחִלַּת יְסוֹדָהּ צָדִ"י, כְּשֶׁהִיא מִתְפַּעֶלֶת ל' מִתְפַּעֵל, אוֹ נִתְפַּעֵל, בָּאָה טֵי"ת בְּתוֹכָהּ וְחוֹלֶקֶת אֶת אוֹתִיוֹת שָׁרְשֵׁי הַתֵּיבָה, כְּמוֹ (בראשית מד טז): מַה נִּצְטַדָּק, מִגִּזְרַת צֶדֶק, שֶׁאוֹמֵר נִּצְטַדַּק, וְכֵן (דניאל ד ל): גִּשְׁמֵהּ יִצְטַבַּע, מִגִּזְרַת צֶבַע.


**They presented themselves as envoys.** They made themselves look like messengers. The word יִצְטַיָרוּ messenger, is from, צִיר “וְצִיר בַּגוֹיִם שְׁלַח” (Ovadiah 1:1) [and a messenger was sent among the nations.] Every word whose root begins with a “צ” (צַדִי ), when used as a reflexive either in the form of מִתְפַּעֵל or נִתְפַּעֵל, is constructed with a “ט” (טֶית) in the middle and separates the root letters of the word,³ like מַה נִצְטַדָק, (how can we justify ourselves).⁴ the root of נִצְטַדָק is צֶדֶק (justice) which he says as נִצְטַדָק. Similarly, וּמִטַל שְׁמַיָא גִשְׁמֵה יִצְטַבַע [His body was wet from the dew of Heaven)⁵ The root of יִצְטַבַע is צֶבַע.

footnotes:
³ Rashi thus explains why the letter “ט” was inserted in the word יִצְטַיָרוּ.
⁴ See Bereishis 44:16.
⁵ Daniel 6:21.


###### Rashi on Joshua 9:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:3)


**שַׂקִּים בָּלִים** שֶׁיִּהְיוּ נִרְאִים כְּבָאִים מֵאֶרֶץ רְחוֹקָה.


**Worn-out sacks.** To give the appearance that they were coming from a distant land.


###### Rashi on Joshua 9:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:4:4)


**וּמְצֹרָרִים** לְשׁוֹן מְבוּקָּעִים, לְשׁוֹן אֲרַמִּי: צִירְיָא דְּחִיטֵּי דְּמָצְרִי זִיקֵי.


**And tied together.** מְצרָרִים is another expression of מְבוּקָעִים (split), which is an Aramaic expression, “The cleft of the wheat.”⁶ of the other, so that the water does not cover it on all sides and it will cause fermentation. “Which makes the wine-skin burst.”⁷

footnotes:
⁶ See Pesachim 40A. Rabbi Yoseph said: A man must not scald two grains of wheat together, lest one becomes wedged in the cleft
⁷ See Avodah Zara 30A. There is a type of wine which is considered “strong wine,” and it is known to make the wine-skin burst. According to most commentaries however, the word מְצרָרִים is translated tied up.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Joshua 9:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:5:1)


**נִקֻּדִים** ארשי"ץ בְּלַעַז (שְׂרוּפִים כֶּעֵין צְנִימִים), לְשׁוֹן מוֹקֵד, וְכֵן תִּרְגְּמוֹ יוֹנָתָן: כִּסְנִין.


**Moldy.** “Arson” in old French is an expression of מוֹקֵד, fire or heat.⁸ Targum Yonasan also translates נִקֻדִים as כִּסְנִין.

footnotes:
⁸ Rashi explains that נִקֻדִים indicates that the bread was not merely dry but burned as toast which would preserve it over a long period. He therefore would reject the translation of נְקֻדִים as moldy.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Joshua 9:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:7:1)


**אוּלַי בְּקִרְבִּי אַתָּה יוֹשֵׁב** שֶׁמָּא מִיּוֹשְׁבֵי הָאָרֶץ אַתֶּם.


**Maybe you really live in our midst.** Perhaps you are of those who dwell in the land.⁹ said, but it is read וַיאמַר אִישׁ “the *man* of Yisroel said.” They all spoke together as a single person who had doubts about the truth of their presentation.

footnotes:
⁹ The Torah explicitly states that the inhabitants of the land of Canaan were to be totally destroyed. It was forbidden to make any sort of treaty with them, as it is said “Do not make a treaty with them or with their gods.” (Shemos 23:32). The men of Givon therefore attempted to portray themselves as people who were not of Canaan and therefore not included in the destruction that was to befall all those who were inhabitants of the land. The verse states וַיאמְרוּ אִישׁ, and they



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Joshua 9:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:12:1)


**הִצְטַיַּדְנוּ** לְשׁוֹן צֵדָה, כְּשֶׁהוֹצֵאנוּהוּ לְצֵדָה לַדֶּרֶךְ.


**We prepared.** The word הִצְטַיַדְנוּ is from צֵדָה provision. When we took [the bread] as provisions for our journey.



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on Joshua 9:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/9:14:1)


**וַיִּקְחוּ הָאֲנָשִׁים מִצֵּידָם** קִבְּלוּ דִּבְרֵיהֶם, שֶׁצָּדוּם בְּפִיהֶם, לְשׁוֹן (שמות כא יג): וַאֲשֶׁר לֹא צָדָה.


**And the men took of their provisions.** They accepted their words. They trapped them with their mouths,¹⁰ as it is said, “But if he did not set an ambush.”¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰ The men of Yisroel were taken in by their trick.
¹¹ Shemos 21:13.



## Chapter 10


### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Joshua 10:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:12:1)


**אָז יְדַבֵּר** אָמַר שִׁירָה תַּחַת הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ, לְפִי שֶׁאָמַר לַשֶּׁמֶשׁ דּוֹם, דּוֹם מִלּוֹמַר שִׁירָה, וְכָל זְמַן שֶׁהוּא דּוֹמֵם, עוֹמֵד וְאֵינוֹ מְהַלֵּךְ, שֶׁבְּכָל עֵת הִילּוּכוֹ הוּא אוֹמֵר שִׁירָה. וּפְשׁוּטוֹ שֶׁל מִקְרָא: דּוֹם לְשׁוֹן הַמְתָּנָה, כְּמוֹ (שמואל א יד ט): אִם כֹּה יֹאמְרוּ אֵלֵינוּ דֹּמּוּ, וְכֵן (תהלים לז ז): דֹּם לַה'.


**Then [Yehoshua] spoke.** He said a song [of praise to God]¹ in place of the sun, for he had said “Let the sun stop.” [meaning] that he silenced it from reciting its song.² During the time it was silent it stood still without moving [on its Orbit,] for during every moment of its orbital movement it recites a song of praise.³ The plain meaning of the verse is that the word דוֹם is an expression of waiting as stated, “If they say to us wait,”⁴ and similarly, “Wait for Adonoy,”⁵

footnotes:
¹ According to Rashi, Yehoshua sang a song of praise, and the word יְדַבֵּר which is usually translated as spoke, in this context is an expression of שִׁירָה.
² According to Rambam and other commentaries, the sun, the moon and other heavenly spheres are living beings and possess intelligence. They hear and speak, and thus they interpret these verses literally: “The heavens recount the glory of the Almighty.” (Tehillim 19:2), and Praise Him, sun and moon, praise Him, all the stars of light. (Tehillim 148:3). They actually verbalize the praises of God in the same manner as do human beings and angels.
³ Every moment of its orbit is a source of praise to God who created it in such a miraculous manner.
⁴ I Shmuel 14:9.
⁵ Tehillim 37:7.


###### Rashi on Joshua 10:12:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:12:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:12:2)


**וְיָרֵחַ בְּעֵמֶק אַיָּלוֹן** אוֹתוֹ פַּעַם הָיָה הַיָּרֵחַ עוֹמֵד כְּנֶגֶד עֵמֶק אַיָּלוֹן, וְהוּא רָחוֹק מִגִּבְעוֹן, שֶׁהֲרֵי גִּבְעוֹן בִּגְבוּל בִּנְיָמִין וְאַיָּלוֹן בִּגְבוּל דָּן.


**And the moon in the valley of Ayalon.** At that moment the moon stood opposite the valley of Ayalon which is a distance from Givon, for Givon is within the border of Binyomin⁶ and Ayalon is within the border of Don.⁷

footnotes:
⁶ Binyomin’s portion was in the south.
⁷ Don’s portion was in the north.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Joshua 10:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:13:1)


**הֲלֹא הִיא כְתוּבָה** דָּבָר זֶה נִכְתַּב בַּתּוֹרָה, שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ יַעֲקֹב לְיוֹסֵף (בראשית מח יט): וְזַרְעוֹ (שֶׁל אֶפְרַיִם) יִהְיֶה מְלֹא הַגוֹיִם, אֵימָתַי, בְּיוֹם שֶׁעָמְדָה חַמָּה לִיהוֹשֻׁעַ, נִתְמַלֵּא כָּל הָעוֹלָם שִׁמְעוֹ שֶׁל יְהוֹשֻׁעַ, וַיַּעֲמֹד הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ בַּחֲצִי הַשָּׁמַיִם וְלֹא אָץ לָבֹא כְּיוֹם תָּמִים.


**Is it not written [in the Seifer Ha Yoshor.]** This subject is recorded in the Torah,⁸ for Yaakov had said to Yosef, regarding the descendents of Ephraim “The fame of his descendants will fill the nations.”⁹ When did this occur? On the day the sun stood still for Yehoshua, the entire world was filled with the fame of Yehoshua.¹⁰ The sun stood still half-way in the sky¹¹ and did not hasten to set for a complete day.

footnotes:
⁸ What is the Seifer Ha Yoshor that is mentioned in this verse? We do not have any book that is called by that name. Rashi identifies the Seifer Ha Yoshor with Seifer Bereishis. It is so called because Yoshor means upright, and this refers to our righteous forefathers, Avrohom, Yitchok and Yaakov.
⁹ Bereishis 48:19.
¹⁰ Yehoshua was a descendant of the tribe of Ephraim.
¹¹ It stood still half-way through its orbit in the sky.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Joshua 10:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:18:1)


**גֹּלּוּ** גַּלְגְּלוּ אֲבָנִים אֶל פִּי הַמְּעָרָה.


**Roll.** Roll large stones to the mouth of the cave.¹²

footnotes:
¹² Rashi explains that the word  גָלוּ has the same meaning as גַלגְלוּ which means roll.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on Joshua 10:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:21:1)


**לֹא חָרַץ לִבְנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל** הֲרֵי זֶה מִקְרָא קָצָר, לֹא חָרַץ הַחוֹרֵץ לְאִישׁ מִבְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל אֶת לְשׁוֹנוֹ.


**Not one person sharpened.** This is an abbreviated verse.¹³ No sharp [tongued] person, sharpened his tongue against a single person of Bnei Yisroel.

footnotes:
¹³ Since the verse does not identify the subject, it is considered an abreviated verse.


###### Rashi on Joshua 10:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:21:2)


**חָרַץ** לְשׁוֹן דִּבּוּר הַלָּשׁוֹן, וְכֵן (שמות יא ז): לֹא יֶחֱרַץ כֶּלֶב לְשׁוֹנוֹ, וְכֵן (שמואל ב ה כד): אָז תֶּחֱרָץ, דְּדָוִד תִּקְרָא, תִּצְעַק קוֹל תְּשׁוּאוֹת הַמִּלְחָמָה.


חָרֶץ indicates the speech of the tongue. Similarly, “A dog will not sharpen its tongue.”¹⁴ Similarly “Then you will utter a sharp word.”¹⁵ You will call, you will cry out a battle shout.

footnotes:
¹⁴ Shemos 11:7 [see Rashi there].
¹⁵ II Shmuel 5:24.



### Verse 40

###### Rashi on Joshua 10:40:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:40:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:40:1)


**וְהָאֲשֵׁדוֹת** מָקוֹם שֶׁמֵּי הַגְּבָעוֹת שׁוֹפְכִים.


**The river valleys.** The place where the water of the hills rush down.



### Verse 41

###### Rashi on Joshua 10:41:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:41:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/10:41:1)


**מִקָּדֵשׁ בַּרְנֵע וְעַד עַזָּה** מִצַּד דְּרוֹמִית שֶׁל אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל הוּא, מִן הַמִּזְרָח לַמַּעֲרָב, וְלֹא הִסְפִּיק לִכְבּוֹשׁ כָּל הַמֶּצֶר וְנִשְׁאַר מֵעַזָּה עַד הַיָּם, הוּא שֶׁאָמַר לְּמַטָּה: זֹאת הָאָרֶץ הַנִּשְׁאֶרֶת הָעַזָּתִי וְהָאַשְׁדּוֹדִי וְגוֹ'.


**From Kadush-barnea to Gaza.** This refers to the south side of Eretz Yisroel going from east to west, but he did not conquer the entire boundary, leaving unconquered the area from Gaza to the [Mediterranean] Sea. This is what is stated below, “This is the land that remained [unconquered], the Gazites and the Ashdodites”¹⁶

footnotes:
¹⁶ Chapter 13:2,3



## Chapter 11


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Joshua 11:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:2:1)


**נֶגֶב כִּנְרוֹת** דְּרוֹם גִּינוֹסָר.


**South of Kinros.**— South of Ginosar.¹

footnotes:
¹ Genosar is an area near Tiberias, around Lake Kinneres.


###### Rashi on Joshua 11:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:2:2)


**וּבְנָפוֹת דּוֹר** פִּלְכֵי דּוֹר, קונטדו"ש בְּלַעַז (חֶלְקֵי אָרֶץ, מְחוֹזוֹת).


**The region of Dor.**—The districts of Dor. Contree in French.


###### Rashi on Joshua 11:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:2:3)


**מִיָּם** מַעֲרָבָה.


מִיָם—on the west.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Joshua 11:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:8:1)


**מִשְׂרְפוֹת מַיִם** תִּרְגֵּם יוֹנָתָן: חֲרִיצֵי יַמָּא, שֶׁעוֹשִׂין חֲרִיצִין וּמֵי הַיָּם יוֹצְאִין לְתוֹכָן, וְנִשְׂרָפִין מֵחוֹם הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ וְנַעֲשִׂים מֶלַח.


**מִשְׂרְפוֹת מַיִם²** Targum Yonasan translates, sea-ditches. They dug ditches and the sea water would flow into them, and the water would be burned [evaporate] from the heat of the sun and would turn into salt.

footnotes:
² From the word שְׂרֵפָה, fire or burning. Thus the Targum proceeds to explain: the burning of water, מַיִם.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on Joshua 11:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:13:1)


**הָעֹמְדוֹת עַל תִּלָּם** שֶׁלֹּא נֶהֱרְסָה חוֹמָתָם כְּמוֹ יְרִיחוֹ שֶׁנָּפְלָה חוֹמָתָהּ, וְכֵן הָעַי שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בָּהּ (לעיל ח כח): וַיְשִׂימֶהָ תֵּל עוֹלָם שְׁמָמָה.


**Remained with their walls intact.** Their walls were not destroyed like Yericho, whose walls fell, and similarly Ai, as it is said, “[And Yehoshua burned Ai] and made it into a mound forever, a ruin.”³

footnotes:
³ See Chapter 8:28 above.


###### Rashi on Joshua 11:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:13:2)


**זוּלָתִי אֶת־חָצוֹר לְבַדָּהּ** בַּמָּסוֹרֶת שְׂרָפָה משֶׁה צִוָּה לוֹ וּמָסַר לוֹ כֵּן. (בראשית רבה וישלח):


**The only exception was Chatzor.** He burned it because of a traditional command. Moshe so commanded and transmitted it to him. This is found in Bereishis Rabba on the Sidrah of “And Yaakov sent.”⁴

footnotes:
⁴ This command to destroy Chatzor is not written in the Torah. Moshe received this command orally on Mount Sinai and transmitted it to Yehoshua, who fulfilled it.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on Joshua 11:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:17:1)


**הָהָר הֶחָלָק** (תַּרְגּוּם:) טוּרָא שְׁעִיעַ, מַחֲלִיק, הָהָר הֶחָלָק עַד בַּעַל גָּד בַּמֶּצֶר הַמִּזְרָחִי, וְלֹא הִסְפִּיק לִכְבּוֹשׁ בְּחַיָּיו אֶת כָּל הַמֶּצֶר עַד הַצָּפוֹן.


**The bare, divided mountain.** A smooth mountain.⁵ This was a bare (smooth) mountain to Baal-gad, which was on the eastern border. During his lifetime, Yehoshua, was unable to conquer the entire eastern border to the north.

footnotes:
⁵ It was so called because there were no trees on it. It was divided between Yaakov and Eisov. The part belonging to Yaakov was bare and smooth as Yaakov himself. See Bereishis 27:11.


###### Rashi on Joshua 11:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:17:2)


**בַּעַל גָּד** מֵישַׁר גָּד.


**Baal-gad**—the plain of gad.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on Joshua 11:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/11:18:1)


**יָמִים רַבִּים עָשָׂה יְהוֹשֻׁעַ** בִּגְנוּתוֹ סִפֵּר הַכָּתוּב, שֶׁהָיָה מִתְכַּוֵּון לִדְחוֹת אֶת כִּבּוּשׁ הָאָרֶץ כְּדֵי לְהַאֲרִיךְ יָמָיו, לְפִי שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (לעיל א י): וְאַתָּה תַּנְחִלֶנָּה אוֹתָם, בְּמִדְרַשׁ תַּנְחוּמָא.


**For many days did Yehoshua.** This verse was said to criticize Yehoshua, who intended to delay the conquest of the land, in order to lengthen his days, for it is said, “And you will apportion it to them.”⁶ Because he was reluctant to complete the conquest, God punished him, and as soon as the apportioning of the land was completed he died. He was then one hundred and ten years old. Were it not for this, he would have lived ten more years, till one hundred and twenty as did his master, Moshe. From *Midrash Tanchumah.*

footnotes:
⁶ It took seven years for Yehoshua to conquer the whole land. Our Sages say that he could have conquered the land more quickly. It did not have to take seven years. Yehoshua was reluctant to complete the conquest of the land and divide it between the tribes. He was afraid that once he completed it, he would die. He knew his mission in life was to bring Bnei Yisroel into Eretz Yisroel and settle them there. Once his mission was completed, his life would have no further purpose. See Devorim 31:7



## Chapter 12


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Joshua 12:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:1:1)


**אֲשֶׁר הִכּוּ בְּנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל** בִּימֵי משֶׁה.


**That the Bnei Yisroel struck.** In the days [lifetime] of Moshe.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Joshua 12:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:2:1)


**וְעַד יַבֹּק הַנַּחַל** שֶׁשָּׁם גְּבוּל אֶרֶץ בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן, סוֹף מֶצֶר אֶרֶץ בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן, וּמִשָּׁם וָהָלְאָה הָיוּ בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן מוֹשְׁלִים.


**To the Yabok river,** which was the border of the land of the Bnei Amon, the end of the territory of the land of Bnei Amon. From there [the Yabok River] and further the Bnei Amon had dominion.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Joshua 12:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:5:1)


**וַחֲצִי הַגִּלְעָד גְּבוּל סִיחוֹן** שָׁם הָיָה מֶצֶר מֶמְשֶׁלֶת סִיחוֹן, כְּשֶׁאָמַרְנוּ לְמַעְלָה, שֶׁחֲצִי הַגִּלְעָד הָיָה שֶׁלּוֹ, וְזֶה חֲצִי הַשֵּׁנִי שֶׁל עוֹג.


**Half of the Gilod, the boundry of Sichon.** That was the border of Sichon’s domain, as we said above,¹ that half of Gilod was his, and this second half was Og’s.

footnotes:
¹ See Verse 2.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on Joshua 12:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/12:23:1)


**לְנָפַת דּוֹר** (תַּרְגּוּם:) לְפַּלְכֵי דּוֹר.


**The Dor region. The provinces of Dor.**



## Chapter 13


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:1:1)


**וְהָאָרֶץ נִשְׁאֲרָה הַרְבֵּה מְאֹד** מִמַּה שֶּׁאָמַרְתִּי לְאַבְרָהָם נִשְׁאֲרָה לְרִשְׁתָּהּ, שֶׁלֹּא נִכְבְּשָׁה.


**A great deal of the land remains—** Of the land I promised to Avrohom, much remains to be possessed [as an inheritance], for it has not been conquered.¹

footnotes:
¹ And since you are old, you will not be able to do so in your lifetime.



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:2:1)


**גְּלִילוֹת** מרקא"ש בְּלַעַז (אֵזוֹרֵי הַגְבוּל, הַסְפַר).


**Borders.** Marche in old French.



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:1)


**מִן הַשִּׁיחוֹר** הוּא נִילוּס, הוּא נַחַל מִצְרַיִם, הוּא סָמוּךְ לִתְחוּם מִקְצוֹעַ דְּרוֹמִית מַעֲרָבִית שֶׁל אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל, כְּמוֹ שֶׁאָמַר בְּ'אֵלֶּה מַסְעֵי' (במדבר לד ה), וְלָמַדְנוּ כָּאן שֶׁלֹּא כָּבַשׁ יְהוֹשֻׁעַ בְּחַיָּיו כָּל מֶצֶר הַדְּרוֹמִי אֶלָּא מִמִּדְבַּר צִין, הוּא הָהָר הֶחָלָק הָעוֹלֶה שֵׂעִירָה עַד הַנִּילוּס, הוּא תְּחוּם עַזָּה הָאָמוּר לְמַעְלָה, מִקָּדֵשׁ בַּרְנֵע עַד עַזָּה.


**From Shichor.** This is the Nile River, which is also referred to as the Egyptian brook. It is close to the southwestern border of Eretz Yisroel, as it is said in Parshas, “These are the journeys.”² From here we learn that in Yehoshua’s lifetime, he did not conquer the entire southern border except from the wilderness of Tzin, which is synonymous with the bare, divided mountain³ that goes up to Seir; to the Nile. This is the boundry of Gaza mentioned above, “From Kodeish-Barneia to Gaza.⁴

footnotes:
² Bamidbar 34:5.
³ See above 11:17.
⁴ See above 10:41.


###### Rashi on Joshua 13:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:2)


**וְעַד גְּבוּל עֶקְרוֹן צָפוֹנָה** הָעֶקְרוֹנִי יוֹשֵׁב עַל הַיָּם וּמִתְפַּשֵּׁט לְצַד הַצָּפוֹן יוֹתֵר מִן הָאֲחֵרִים.


**To the border of Ekron, to the north.** The Ekronites lived near the Sea, and they spread out northward further than the others.


###### Rashi on Joshua 13:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:3)


**לַכְּנַעֲנִי תֵּחָשֵׁב** מֵאֶרֶץ הַכְּנַעֲנִי שֶׁנָּתַתִּי לְאַבְרָהָם הוּא.


**Which is considered the territory of Canaan.** It is of the land of Canaan that I gave to Avrohom [as an inheritance].


###### Rashi on Joshua 13:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:4)


**חֲמֵשֶׁת סַרְנֵי פְּלִשְׁתִּים וְגוֹ'** הִיא סוֹף גְּמַר הַמֶּצֶר עַד הַיָּם הַגָּדוֹל שֶׁבַּמַּעֲרָב.


**The territory of the five Philistine rulers.** This is the territory from the end of the border to the Great [Mediterranean] Sea, westward.


###### Rashi on Joshua 13:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:3:5)


**הָעַזָּתִי וְהָאַשְׁדּוֹדִי הָאֶשְׁקְלוֹנִי וְגוֹ'** שִׁשָּׁה סְרָנִים הוּא מוֹנֶה, וּמִתְּחִלָתָן קְרָאָן 'חֲמֵשֶׁת'. אָמַר רַבִּי יוֹנָתָן (חולין ס ב), אִידוֹנְרִיקֵי שֶׁלָּהֶם חַמִשָּׁה; הַחֲשׁוּבִים חֲמִשָּׁה הָיוּ, שֶׁהָעַוִּים אֵינוֹ מוֹנֶה מִן הַסְּרָנִים הַחֲשׁוּבִים. וְעוֹד יֵשׁ לְפָרֵשׁ, חֲמֵשֶׁת סַרְנֵי פְּלִשְׁתִּים: הָעַזָּתִי, וְהָאַשְׁדּוֹדִי, הָאֶשְׁקְלוֹנִי, הַגִּתִּי וְהָעֶקְרוֹנִי הֵם חֲמִשָּׁה, וְעוֹד נִשְׁאַר לִכְבּוֹשׁ אֶרֶץ הָעַוִּים שֶׁאֵינָם מִן הַפְּלִשְׁתִּים.


**The Azites, the Ashdodites, the Ashkelonites.** There are six rulers counted in the verse and he previously called them five [rulers]. Said Rav Yochanan, “Five of them were prominent. There were five prominent rulers [that were counted], for the Avites were not counted among the prominent rulers.⁵ It can be explained in an other way: The five Philistine rulers were: the Azites, the Ashdodites, the Ashkelonites, the Gittites, and the Ekronites. These are the five [rulers mentioned]. The land of the Avites remained unconquered for they were not of the Philistine people.

footnotes:
⁵ Maseches Chulin 60b.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:4:1)


**מִתֵּימָן כָּל אֶרֶץ הַכְּנַעֲנִי** פָּסוּק רִאשׁוֹן מָנָה מִצַּד הַדְּרוֹמִי מִן הַמִּזְרָח לַמַּעֲרָב, וְזֶה מוֹנֶה אֶת רוֹחַב אוֹתוֹ הַמִּקְצוֹעַ הַנּוֹתָר לִיכָּבֵשׁ, כַּמָּה הוּא מִן הַדָּרוֹם לַצָּפוֹן.


**From the south, all the land of Canaanites.** The preceding verse described the southern border going from east to west, and this verse describes the width of that area that remained to be conquered; describing how much there was from south to north.


###### Rashi on Joshua 13:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:4:2)


**מִתֵּימָן כָּל אֶרֶץ הַכְּנַעֲנִי** מִתֵּימָן זוֹ מְעָרָה לַצִּידוֹנִים, עַד אֲפֵקָה עַד הָאֱמוֹרִי.


**From the south, all the land of the Canaanites.** From the south which is Gaza belonging to the Sidonites, to Apheik, to the Emorites.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:5:1)


**וְהָאָרֶץ הַגִּבְלִי וְכָל הַלְּבָנוֹן מִזְרַח הַשֶּׁמֶשׁ** וּבַמֶּצֶר מִזְרָחִי נִשְׁאָר לִכְבּוֹשׁ בְּמִקְצוֹעַ הַצָּפוֹן כָּל הַלְּבָנוֹן, מִבַּעַל גָּד עַד סוֹף הַמֶּצֶר, וּבָרוֹחַב מִן הַמִּזְרָח לַמַּעֲרָב עַד לְבוֹא חֲמָת, זֶהוּ כָּל מֶצֶר הַצְּפוֹנִי שֶׁלְּבוֹא חֲמָת בְּמִקְצוֹעַ צְפוֹנִית מַעֲרָבִית בְּ'אֵלֶּה מַסְעֵי' (במדבר לד ח).


**The land of the Givlites and the entire Lebanon eastward foward the rising sun.** And on the eastern border, the northern end remained unconquered, [including] the entire Lebanon, from Baal-gad, to the end of the border; and in the width from east to west to Levo-Chamos. This is the entire northern border, for Levo-Chamos is at the northwestern corner, as stated in Parshas “These are the journeys.”⁶

footnotes:
⁶ Bamidbar 34:7,8.

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:6:1)


**אָנֹכִי אוֹרִישֵׁם** אַחֲרֵי מוֹתְךָ.


**I will evict them—** after your death.


###### Rashi on Joshua 13:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:6:2)


**הַפִּלֶהָ לְיִשְׂרָאֵל בְּנַחֲלָה** וְיִכְבּוֹשׁ לְאַחַר זְמָן כָּל שֵׁבֶט וְשֵׁבֶט מַה שֶּׁנָּפַל בְּגוֹרָלוֹ.


**Cast lots to divide it as an inheritance to Bnei Yisroel—** and later they will conquer, each tribe [will conquer] that area which fell in its lot.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:8:1)


**עִמּוֹ הָראוּבֵנִי וְהַגָּדִי** עִם חֲצִי הָרִאשׁוֹן שֶׁל מְנַשֶּׁה, הָראוּבֵנִי וְהַגָּדִי לָקְחוּ נַחֲלָתָם.


**With him, the tribes of Reuvein and Gad.** With the first half of the tribe of Menashe, the tribes of Reuvein and Gad took their inheritance.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:9:1)


**מֵעֲרוֹעֵר אֲשֶׁר עַל שְׂפַת וְגוֹ'** מוֹנֶה וְהוֹלֵךְ כָּל אֶרֶץ עֵבֶר הַיַּרְדֵּן, וְאַחַר כָּךְ מְפָרֵשׁ גְּבוּל כָּל שֵׁבֶט וְשֵׁבֶט וְיִתֵּן משֶׁה לְמַטֶּה פְּלוֹנִי וּפְלוֹנִי.


**From Aroreir, which is on the bank etc.** He procedes to describe the entire land on the other side of the Jordan. Then he explains the boundry of each tribe; [as stated], “And Moshe gave”⁷—i.e., he gave to each tribe [their territory].

⁷ Below V. 15.



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/13:12:1)


**מִיֶּתֶר הָרְפָאִים** שֶׁהָרַג כְּדָרְלָעֹמֶר וְהַמְּלָכִים אֲשֶׁר אִתּוֹ, כְּמָה שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר (בראשית יד ה): וַיַּכּוּ אֶת רְפָאִים בְּעַשְׁתְּרוֹת קַרְנַיִם.


**From the remnant of the giants—** who were slain by Kedorlaomeir and the kings who were aligned with him, as it is said, “And they struck the Rephaim in Ashteros Karnayim.”⁸

footnotes:
⁸ Bereishis 14:5.

**בְּהַר הָעֵמֶק** (תַּרְגּוּם:) בְּטוּרָא דְמֵישָׁרָא.


**On the Mountain of the valley.** The mountain of the plain.

**And half the land of the Bnei Amon.** Half the land they conquered from the Bnei Amon and from Sichon.

###### Rashi on Joshua 13:27:1

**The Jordan and its border.** The Jordan and its boundary, [including] the cities which are on its bank.

**And their villages.** The open cities without walls.



## Chapter 14


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Joshua 14:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:1:1)


**אֲשֶׁר נָחֲלוּ אוֹתָם** אֲשֶׁר הִנְחִילוּ אוֹתָם.


**That were apportioned to them.** They caused them to inherit [their portions].



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on Joshua 14:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:4:1)


**כִּי הָיוּ בְּנֵי יוֹסֵף שְׁנֵי מַטּוֹת** תַּחַת שֵׁבֶט לֵוִי.


**The descendants of Yoseif were two tribes.** In place of the tribe of Levi.¹ were not to be given a portion of the land. The land was to be divided only among the other eleven tribes. The children of Menashe and Ephraim were counted as two tribes so that the land would be divided by twelve tribes. This came about through the will of Yaakov, who told Yoseif that his two sons, Ephraim and Menashe, would be considered as his own two son, Reuven and Shimon. (See Bereishis 48:7) This is mentioned here to explain how Yehoshua was to divide the land among nine and half tribes, when without Levi, there would be only eight and a half. We are told therefore that Menashe and Ephraim were counted as two tribes, thus this would compensate for the omission of Levi.

footnotes:
¹ Moshe and Yehoshua were commanded that the Levi’im



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on Joshua 14:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:7:1)


**כַּאֲשֶׁר עִם לְבָבִי** וְלֹא כַּאֲשֶׁר עִם פִּי, שֶׁהַמְרַגְּלִים הָיוּ בְּעֵצָה אַחַת, וְיָּרֵא כָּלֵב לוֹמַר לָהֶם שֶׁלֹּא יֹאמַר כְּמוֹתָם, וּכְשֶׁבָּא הִכְחִישָׁם, וְזֶהוּ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר שָׁם (במדבר יד כד): עֵקֶב הָיְתָה רוּחַ אַחֶרֶת עִמּוֹ, שֶׁהָיָה אוֹמֵר לָהֶם אֶחָד בַּפֶּה וְאֶחָד בַּלֵּב.


**As was in my heart.** But not as it was in my mouth, for the spies were united in their scheme and Caleiv was afraid to tell them that he would not say as they would.² But when he returned [to Moshe] he contradicted them.³ This is what is stated there, “Because he possesed in him a different spirit,”⁴ for what he said to them by mouth was not what was in his heart.

footnotes:
² It is difficult to resist group pressure. As long as he was with the other spies, he hid his true feelings, because he wanted them to think that he agreed with them. Otherwise, he was afraid that they would harm him.
³ By the time Caleiv returned to Moshe, he said with all his heart, “The land through which we passed to scout it, that land is very, very good. If Adonoy desires us, He will bring us into the land and give it to us.” (Bamidbar 13:7,8).
⁴ Bamidbar 14:24.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on Joshua 14:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:10:1)


**זֶה אַרְבָּעִים וְחָמֵשׁ שָׁנָה** לָמַדְנוּ שֶׁשָּׁהוּ בְּכִיבּוּשׁ הָאָרֶץ שֶׁבַע שָׁנִים, שֶׁהֲרֵי בַּשָּׁנָה הַשֵּׁנִית שָׁלַח משֶׁה הַמְרַגְּלִים, נִשְׁאֲרוּ שְׁלשִׁים וּשְׁמֹנֶה שֶׁהָלְכוּ בַּמִּדְבָּר וְשֶׁבַע שֶׁכָּבְשׁוּ, הֲרֵי אַרְבָּעִים וְחָמֵשׁ.


**It is now forty five years.** From this we learn that it took seven years to conquer the land, for it was in the second year⁵ that Moshe sent the spies.⁶ After that, there remained thirty eight years during which they traveled through the wilderness, and seven years to conquer the land, which add up to forty five years.

footnotes:
⁵ After they went out of Egypt.
⁶ To spy out the land.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on Joshua 14:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:15:1)


**הָאָדָם הַגָּדוֹל בָּעֲנָקִים הוּא** אֲבִיהֶם שֶׁל אֲחִימָן שֵׁשַׁי וְתַלְמַי, אַרְבַּע הָיָה שְׁמוֹ. דָּבָר אַחֵר: עַל שֵׁם הָאָב וּשְׁלשָׁה בָּנִים, שֶׁכֵּן קוֹרֵא אוֹתָם (שם יג כב): יְלִידֵי הָעֲנָק.


**The greatest man among the giants.** The father of Achimon, Sheishai, and Talmai was Arba.⁷ Another explanation: [It was called Kiryas-arba, the city of four]⁸ because of the father and the three sons, for they are referred to as the offspring of the “Anak,” [the giant].⁹

footnotes:
⁷ Chevron was the home of these four famous giants, the three brothers and their father.
⁸ There are other interpretations regarding what the “four” refers to. Some say it refers to the burial place of four great men and their wives: Odom and Chava, Avrohom and Soroh, Yitzchok and Rivkoh, and Yaakov and Leah.
⁹ See Bamidbar 13:22.


###### Rashi on Joshua 14:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/14:15:2)


**וְהָאָרֶץ שָׁקְטָה מִמִּלְחָמָה** מוּסָב לָעִנְיָן הָרִאשׁוֹן, לְאַחַר שֶׁבַע שֶׁכָּבְשׁוּ סְבִיבוֹתֵיהֶם, נִכְנְעוּ הָאֱמוֹרִיִים וְלֹא נֶאֶסְפוּ עוֹד לְמִלְחָמָה עֲלֵיהֶם, לְכָךְ הִתְחִילוּ לַעֲסוֹק בְּחִילּוּק הָאָרֶץ. וּמִדְרַשׁ אַגָּדָה: הָאָדָם הַגָּדוֹל בָּעֲנָקִים הוּא אַבְרָהָם אָבִינוּ, הוּא שֶׁגָּרַם לָהֶם שֶׁהָאָרֶץ שָׁקְטָה מִמִּלְחָמָה אַרְבָּעִים שָׁנָה שֶׁנִּתְעַכְּבוּ בַּמִּדְבָּר, בְּשָׂכָר שֶׁכִּבְּדוּ אֶת הַזָּקֵן בְּקִרְיַת אַרְבַּע, שֶׁאָמְרוּ לוֹ (בראשית כג ו): נְשִׂיא אֱלֹהִים אַתָּה בְּתוֹכֵנוּ.


**And the land had respite from war.** This refers back to the previous subject.¹⁰ After the seven years of conquest, the Emorites were humbled and they no longer gathered for war against them. Therefore they began to be occupied in the division of the land [among the tribes] the Midrash Aggadah comments that the greatest man among giants refers to our father, Avrohom. Because of him, the land [of the Canaanite people,] had respite from war during the forty years [that Bnei Yisroel] lingered in the wilderness. They were so rewarded because they honored the Patriarch in Kiryas-arba, when they said to him, “You are a prince of God in our midst.”¹¹

footnotes:
¹⁰ See above 11:23. The conclusion of the verse does not seem connected to the subject matter of Caleiv’s receiving Chevron as his inheritance. Rashi, therefore comments that indeed, it refers to the earlier topic discussed in the previous chapter.
¹¹ Bereishis 23:6.



## Chapter 15


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on Joshua 15:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:1:1)


**אֶל גְּבוּל אֱדוֹם** סָמוּךְ לְמֶצֶר אֱדוֹם.


**To the border of Edom.** Close to the boundary of Edom.


###### Rashi on Joshua 15:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:1:2)


**נֶגְבָּה** בִּתְחוּם מֶצֶר דְּרוֹמִי שֶׁל אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל.


**Southward.** Referring to the southern boundary of Eretz Yisroel.¹

¹ The wilderness of Tzin borders the territory of Yehudah on the south.


###### Rashi on Joshua 15:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:1:3)


**מִקְצֵה תֵימָן** בְּסוֹף כָּל הַמֶּצֶר.


**The extreme southern end.** Referring to the very end of the boundary.

###### Rashi on Joshua 15:2:1

**וַיְהִי לָהֶם גְּבוּל** מֶצֶר דְּרוֹמִי שֶׁל יְהוּדָה.


**And their southern border was.** Referring to the southern border of Yehudah.


###### Rashi on Joshua 15:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20Joshua/r/15:2:2)


**מִקְצֵה יַם הַמֶּלַח** הוּא מִקְצוֹעַ דְּרוֹמִית מִזְרָחִית שֶׁל אֶרֶץ יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּ'אֵלֶּה מַסְעֵי' (במדבר לד ג).


**From the end of the Salt (Dead) Sea.** Referring to the southeastern corner of Eretz Yisroel as stated in *Parshas* אֵלֶה מַסְעֵי²
